1. From S, we can directly go to E, B, or F.
2. From T, we can directly go to B or F.

- Path 1: S → E → B → T (Length: 3)

   - E -> B -> J
   - E -> B -> H -> J
   - E -> B -> N -> J
   - E -> B -> S -> T -> G -> R -> Q -> I -> J
   - E -> B -> S -> F -> C -> G -> R -> Q -> I -> J
   - E -> B -> S -> F -> P -> O -> Q -> I -> J

   - **N**: Direct connection between B and J.
   - **H**: Direct connection between B and J.
   - **S**: Connects E to multiple paths leading to J.
   - **F**: Connects S to multiple paths leading to J.
   - **C**: Connects F to G, which leads to J.
   - **G**: Connects C to R, which leads to J.
   - **R**: Connects G to Q, which leads to J.
   - **Q**: Connects R to I, which leads to J.
   - **I**: Direct connection to J.

3. **Determine the minimum cut set:**
   - Removing **B** will disrupt all paths that go through B.
   - Additionally, removing **I** will ensure that there are no remaining paths from E to J.

By removing nodes B and I, all paths between E and J are disrupted.
